Let's outline robust optimizer:
- Represent a as float64 length n, simplex.
- Use FFT to compute convolution c and gradients for selected constraints efficiently.
- Objective score = 2n * max(c). We can just minimize M=max(c).
- Since n fixed, scaling constant irrelevant for direction. For final score, compute.

Optimization methods:
1. Smoothing with log-sum-exp with adaptively increasing α. Minimize F_α(a)=logsumexp(α c_k)/α * 2n. Gradient = (∑ e^{α c_k} ∇c_k)/(∑ e^{α c_k}). This uses all constraints weighted by exp. This is stable if α*M ~ maybe 1000; M ~1e-4, can set α up to 1e5. But weights may under/overflow; shift by max. The gradient is dense weighted sum over 2n constraints; computing all gradients naively O(n*2n). But we can compute weighted convolution of weights? For each a, objective smooth: φ = (1/α) log ∑ e^{α c_k}. Gradient wrt a_j = ∑_k w_k ∂c_k/∂a_j = ∑_k w_k (a_{k-j} + a_{j-k}) with bounds. This is like two convolutions between a and w? Let's derive.
For each k, ∂c_k/∂a_j = a_{k-j} if in range + a_{j-k} if in range. Weighted sum over k: ∑ w_k a_{k-j} + ∑ w_k a_{j-k}. The first is convolution? Define w indexed 0..2n-2. For fixed j, sum_k w_k a_{k-j} where k-j in [0,n-1] -> k in j..j+n-1 -> ∑_{r=0}^{n-1} w_{j+r} a_r. That's cross-correlation of w with a: (w ⋆ a)_j? Second ∑ w_k a_{j-k}: r = j-k -> k=j-r, range 0..2n-2, r 0..n-1 -> ∑_{r=0}^{n-1} w_{j-r} a_r (if valid). So gradient = conv(w, a) + conv? Let's test with full linear convolution. If w_k = e^{α c_k}/Z. The gradient of logsumexp c_k = ∑ w_k ∇c_k. Could compute as: 
D = np.convolve(w, a) gives length 3n-3? Let's index. (w * a)_t = ∑_k w_k a_{t-k}. Need for j: first term ∑_r w_{j+r} a_r =? This is cross-correlation w with a. In numpy, np.convolve(w, a) index t: ∑ w_i a_{t-i}. Set t? For t=2n-2 - j? Let's not. We can use FFT to compute all gradients vector length n:
grad_j = ∑_{r=0}^{n-1} a_r (w_{j+r} + w_{j-r}?) with valid ranges. This is a "sliding window sum of w" at offsets ±r. It can be computed as (w * a) + (w_rev? * a)? Let's derive properly using zero-padding.
Let a length n, w length 2n-1. Define B = np.convolve(w, a) length 3n-3? Indices t=0..3n-4: B_t = ∑_{i=0}^{2n-2} w_i a_{t-i}. For first term T1_j=∑_{r=0}^{n-1} w_{j+r} a_r. Set t = j? Let's derive subgradient active weights. At a, let c_k and gradients g_k (n vectors). For max f, subdiff = conv{g_k: active}. However, the smooth log-sum-exp gradient is a weighted average with weights almost optimal (maximizes sum exp), maybe good. It emphasizes the largest c and if many near active, distributes. We can tune α. This uses all constraints, not just top 3. Previous top_k=3 maybe too few; if many active peaks, average over few may mislead. Gradient computation via FFT:
```
def conv(a,b): return fftconvolve(a,b)
def logsumexp_grad(a, alpha):
    c = conv(a,a)  # length 2n-1
    m = c.max()
    w = exp(alpha*(c-m)); w/=w.sum()
    # grad for φα (without α? derivative of logsumexp is ∑w g; derivative of /α is (1/α)∑w g)
    w_rev = w[::-1]
    C1 = conv(a, w)     # length 3n-3
    C2 = conv(a, w_rev) # len 3n-3
    grad = C1[:n] + C2[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    return grad/alpha
```

Gradient of logsumexp:
```
def smooth_gradient(a, alpha):
    c = conv(a,a)
    # if c all zero? 
    cm = c.max()
    w = np.exp(alpha*(c-cm))
    ws = w.sum()
    if not finite or ws==0: uniform weights? 
    w /= ws
    w_rev = w[::-1]
    C = conv(a, w_rev) # length 3n-3
    grad = C[n-1:2*n-1][::-1] * 2.0
    return grad, cm
```
Objective φ = (log(sum e^{αc}) - α cm)/α? Actually if use weights normalized by e^{-αcm}, φ = cm + log(sum w_unscaled)/α. Gradient independent of cm shift: ∫ w ∇c = (1/α)∑w? Option A: FISTA on smooth log-sum-exp with adaptive α and step. Let's try to make robust.

Define `smooth_obj_grad(a, alpha)` returns grad_direction = ∇φ (without 2n constant). Actually φ = (1/α) logsumexp(α c). We can compute w as above, T = conv(a,w_rev)[slice reversed], grad = (2/α) T. But we can use direction `g = 2*T` and step `step/alpha`? Simpler compute grad_phi directly:
```
w = exp(alpha*(c-cm))/sum
grad = 2*T / alpha
```
For α large, grad small? cM~1e-4, α=1e5, T~? If weights on peaks, grad comp ~1/(α n)? very small. Step should scale with α. Maybe optimize unsmoothed log-sumexp `L = logsumexp(α c)`; gradient =2*T*α? Wait L grad = α∑w∇c = α*2T. φ grad =2T. If we minimize L, step smaller. We can choose to minimize φ with grad=2T/α and α large -> gradient tiny. But FISTA step can be based on smoothness L_φ≈? It may scale with α? Let's instead minimize `F = 2n φ`, gradient =2n*2T/α. Scale irrelevant if adaptive. We can use `g = 2*T` (which is ∇ logsumexp) and do projected gradient with step. This g corresponds to gradient of logsumexp (not divided by α). Its magnitude maybe O(1/n). Step size similar to previous top-k average. α only affects weights, not gradient scale. This is convenient: We minimize logsumexp(α c), which is smoother for moderate α but has larger Hessian; however g scale O(1/n). As α increases, g still O(1/n) but concentrates. Use step ~0.2-1.

Is minimizing logsumexp with increasing α equivalent to max? Yes. We can run projected gradient/FISTA on `L_α(c)=log∑exp(α c)`. Its gradient = α∑w∇c = α*2T. If we use `g=2T` (without α), then we are effectively minimizing φ with step not adjusted? The update a - step*2T corresponds to gradient of φ (since grad φ=2T/α?) Wait grad φ = (1/α)*α∑w∇c? Let's calculate: L=logsumexp(αc) = log∑e^{αc}. ∇L = α∑w ∇c = α*2T. φ = L/α. ∇φ=∑w∇c=2T. Ah! So g=2T is gradient of smoothed φ, independent of α. Great! Values of φ not used, direction good. As α increases, weights sharpen, g is a subgradient of max. Step should be for φ, whose Hessian depends on α (can be large). But we can adapt step.

We can use updating `a = project_simplex(y - step * g)` where g=2T/?? maybe multiply by 2n for score? Direction scale same if max step adjusted. Previous top_k average gradient of c (not /α) used step 0.35. We'll use similar.

Minimize `U_p(a) = (∑ c_k^p)^{1/p}` for p≥1. c_k nonnegative. Objective scale: score=2n U_p. For p large approximates max. Gradient: ∂U_p/∂a_j = (1/p) S^{1/p -1} ∑ p c_k^{p-1} ∂c_k/∂a_j = S^{1/p -1} ∑ c_k^{p-1} ∂c_k/∂a_j. Direction weights w_k ∝ c_k^{p-1}; normalized? Then gradient of **normalized p-norm**? Minimize (mean c^p)^{1/p} = S^{1/p}/N^{1/p}. Direction ∝ T / S^{(p-1)/p}. Multiplying by positive scalar doesn't change direction if we normalize weights; but gradient magnitude changes. For projected gradient with adaptive step, direction with normalized weights is fine; it emphasizes peaks. This is like log-sum but polynomial. p can be 2,4,10,50. For p=2, weights c, smooth quadratic sum; for large p, max.

Maybe minimizing **sum of p-th powers** of coefficients directly: minimize Q=∑c_k^p. Gradient direction T with weights c^{p-1}. This may be easier (smooth, no log) and can use L-BFGS. As p→∞, max. Let's see: Q=mean(c^p). ∂Q/∂a_j = (2/p) L^{-1} ∑ p c_k^{p-1} ∂c_k/∂a_j? = (2/L) ∑ c_k^{p-1} ∂c_k = (4/L) T? The constant irrelevant. Could use weights v=c^p normalized to sum1: then gradient direction of **log of sum c^p**? L=log∑c^p, weights v/∑v, grad direction=2T. This is similar log-sum-exp but polynomial; p large. Log-sum-exp more stable? polynomial c^p underflows for c differences; but we can use logs.

Log-sum-exp with α is equivalent to p-norm with p=1+1/α? Not exactly. It is robust.

Let's implement `smooth_grad(a, temp)` where `temp` inverse temperature α. Use α schedule: start 1000, increase to 50000. For each α, run e.g. 500-2000 iterations. Also use multiple α values.

Maybe use **projected gradient with metric (Bregman)**. Since simplex, KL projection can be natural for probabilities: update `a_new ∝ a * exp(-η g)`, maintain sum. This is mirror descent; often good for positive sequences. Formula: given a, subgradient g, choose step η, compute `log a - η g`, exponentiate, normalize. Then use **projected gradient with backtracking on exact score**. Let's detail a robust loop:
```
def optimize(start, n, time_limit, base_alpha, ...):
    a = normalize(start)
    best_score = fast_score(a)
    step = 1.0  # for g direction
    max_step=10.0; min_step=1e-3
    gamma = initial softness
    stuck=0
    momentum=0.9
    prev=None
    L_cache? 
    while time < limit:
       # occasionally increase gamma (sharper) or reset
       if best_score close target: gamma*=1.05
       # compute y with momentum
       if prev is None: y=a.copy()
       else: y = a + momentum*(a-prev); y=np.maximum(y,0); sy=y.sum(); if sy>0: y/=sy
       c, M, w = get_weights(y, gamma)
       g = gradient_from_weights(y,w)  # 2T
       gn = np.linalg.norm(g)
       if gn < 1e-15: break
       d = g/gn  # unit gradient
       # line search steps: try step_sequence relative to norm? Use step_norm = step*gn? 
```
If we normalize g, step has L2 length units. We can set `base_step = 0.05` (L2 change per iteration). For n=4806, a has L2 norm sqrt(∑a_i²) ~maybe 0.01. Update `a_trial=project(y - tau*d)`. This self-scales.
- Compute sc_trial. If sc_trial < sc_y - tol (or sc_y), accept. If accepted, maybe increase tau_fraction*=1.1. Else reduce *=0.5. If no acceptance after e.g. 3 trials, reset momentum, maybe perturb best.
- But computing sc_y: we have c from y, so score_y = 2n*M/(y.sum=1)^2. Good.

- Keep global best.

Let's think of a different algorithm: **Multiplicative Weight Updates (MWA)** for minimization of max polynomial. For maximization of minimum? We can view constraints c_k ≤ M. For current M, choose active constraints, update a_i ∝ exp(-η ∑ active ∂c_k/∂a_i). This is mirror descent. This can be efficient: `z = log(a) - η g`, normalize. It automatically handles nonnegativity and sum. Step η can be chosen. This might outperform Euclidean projection for nonneg. Let's incorporate a **mirror descent variant** as one of optimizers:
- Ensure a positive (add eps). Maintain z=log(a) (or unnormalized). At each iter: compute g (active gradient). Update `z = z - step*g` (maybe with momentum in z). Then `a = softmax(z)`. But softmax will shrink differences if step small. We can use `a = a * exp(-step*g) / sum` (multiplicative). If g_i positive on variables to decrease, their a_i decrease. Step choose. This is like exponentiated gradient. It can be powerful.

We can run a separate optimizer `mirror_descent`:
```

step=1.0
while time:

But z changes with accepted a: set z=log(a). Could get stuck near boundary because log zeros. We can use `a_t = (a * exp(-t*g)); a_t/=sum`. If g_i huge for some i, may zero. Add floor.

Let's implement FFT conv carefully for speed:

```
def fft_convolve(a,b):
    target = len(a)+len(b)-1
    L = 1
    while L < target: L <<=1
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    B = np.fft.rfft(b, L)
    return np.fft.irfft(A*B, L, out?)  # irfft doesn't have out? can use np.fft.irfft.
```
This allocates. For many calls, we can cache L for given length and maybe use `np.fft.rfft` each time. Could precompute L for n and grad. But helper top-level. Function `score_conv(a)`:

L = 1 << ((2*len(a)-2).bit_length())

s = a.sum()

```

Let's implement each carefully.

`fft_conv_score`:
```
def fft_conv_score(a):
    n=a.size
    L=1 << ((2*n-2).bit_length())
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    C=np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)[:2*n-1]
    # small negative due to roundoff
    C=np.maximum(C,0.0) # since nonnegative, can reduce max? But negative small no; max positive
    s=a.sum()
    M=C.max()
    if s <= 0: return float('inf')
    return float(2.0*n*M/(s*s)), C, M
```
Use `np.fft.irfft` full length. A*A real? A complex square; irfft okay. Might introduce tiny negative; max unaffected. No need maxclip (could distort if all negative? no).

`smooth_data(a, eta)`:
We need c and weights and gradient. Use L for gradient (3n-3) which also covers c. But if we already have c from score (Lsmall), computing A at Lsmall and then W at Lbig? For gradient, need FFT of a at Lbig. Could compute separately. In main loop, for current a, compute c with Lbig too to reuse A. But for trial scores use Lsmall.
```
def smooth_data(a, eta):
    n=a.size
    Lg=1 << ((3*n-3).bit_length())
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,Lg)
    C=np.fft.irfft(A*A,Lg)[:2*n-1]
    M=C.max()
    # weights
    if M <= 0: w=uniform
    else:
       # eta = alpha*M? Use delta=M/eta
       w=np.exp((C-M)*(eta/M if M>0 else 0))
       s=w.sum()
       if not finite: fallback top_k uniform
       w/=s
    W=np.fft.rfft(w,Lg)
    T=np.fft.irfft(W * np.conj(A), Lg)[:n]
    grad=2.0*T
    score=2*n*M  # assuming sum=1; if not general: 2n*M/(a.sum()^2)
    return score, M, grad
```
If sequence not sum1, normalize before. For general score, use sum.
Eta (softness) larger -> sharper: weight = exp(eta*(C-M)/M). If M=0, uniform. 
Potential overflow: (C-M) ≤0, exponent ≤0, safe. Underflow to 0 for difference large; okay. If all underflow except max, w one-hot. w sum maybe 1. If M extremely small? c normalized M~1e-4; okay. If M=0 (all zero), fallback.

Is gradient `2*T` with w normalized sum1? Yes. If w one-hot at peak k, T_j = a_{k-j} if valid; grad=2 a_{k-j}. This matches subgradient of c_k (factor? For c_k derivative, if k even diagonal double; our T one-hot: ∑_{r} w_k a_r for r, gradient=2 a_r for r such that r + ? T_j=∑ a_r w_{j+r}; one-hot k: T_j=a_{k-j} if valid. grad=2a_{k-j}. But actual derivative of c_k: for k=0, c0=a0^2 derivative 2a0; our one-hot k=0: T0=a0, grad2a0 good. k=2: derivative 2a0 for c2 includes 2a0a2? Wait c2 derivative wrt a0=2a2; our T0 for k=2: a_{2}? yes 2a2. Derivative wrt a1: T1=a1, grad2a1 (c2 a1^2 derivative 2a1). Good.

Return `grad` of φ (smooth weighted). If we use exact line search, okay.

Subgradient refine algorithm:
```
def subgradient_refine(a0, n, budget, rng, eta_start=5, eta_end=200):
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget
    a=normalize(a0)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=fast_score(a)
    eta=eta_start
    step_frac=0.05
    max_frac=1.0; min_frac=1e-4
    momentum=0.75
    prev=None
    stuck=0
    it=0
    # maybe phase: first run with eta sequence
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # update eta slowly based on time or it
       if it % 100 ==0: eta = min(eta_end, eta*1.02)
       # build y with momentum
       if prev is None:
          y=a.copy()
       else:
          y=a + momentum*(a-prev)
          y=np.maximum(y,0)
          sy=y.sum(); if sy<1e-15: y=a.copy(); else: y/=sy
       score_y, M, grad = smooth_data(y, eta)
       if not finite: ...
       gn=np.linalg.norm(grad)
       if gn < 1e-18: break
       d = -grad/gn
       # line search fractions relative to a's norm
       anorm = np.linalg.norm(a)  # maybe ~0.01
       vals = [step_frac]
       # try up to 4
       accepted=False
       current=score_y
       for frac in [step_frac, step_frac*0.5, step_frac*0.25, step_frac*0.125]:
           tau = frac * max(anorm, 1e-6)
           cand = y + tau*d  # since d neg grad
           cand = project_simplex(cand)
           sc, C, _ = fft_conv_score(cand)
           if sc < current - 1e-14:
               accepted=True; a_new=cand; sc_new=sc; break
       if not accepted:
           # maybe try very small tau
           tau = min_frac * max(anorm, 1e-12)
           cand=project_simplex(y+tau*d); sc=...
           if sc < current - 1e-15: accepted True ...
           else:
               # reduce step, reset momentum, maybe perturb from best if stuck
               step_frac *=0.7
               if step_frac < min_frac:
                   a = best_a.copy(); prev=a.copy(); momentum=0.0; step_frac=0.05
                   # perturb
                   a += rng.normal(0, 1e-4/sqrt(n), n); project
                   it=0; stuck=0
                   continue
               a = best_a.copy() if best_sc<current? But for iteration, set a=a? If no improvement, set a=prev? 
               stuck +=1
               if stuck>50: reset to best perturbed
               # set a to current y? Could keep a old to avoid worsening
               # For momentum, set prev=a_old; a unchanged
       else:
           step_frac = min(max_frac, step_frac*1.05)
           a_new = ...
           # update best
           if sc_new < best_sc - 1e-12:
               best_sc=sc_new; best_a=a_new.copy(); stuck=0
           else:
               stuck +=1
           prev=a; a=a_new; it+=1
       # if stuck large, increase max/eta
```
Need manage loop correctly. Note `score_y` is at y (momentum point) which may not be current a. If line search from y and no improvement relative to y, we shouldn't set a to y. Keep old a. This is accepted but momentum may accumulate. If no accept after some, reset.

Maybe simpler: Use y=a (no momentum) for line search; accepted updates from a. Add momentum later. No momentum is robust. But acceleration can speed. We can use **heavy ball** but with accepted a; if no accept, a unchanged, velocity zero.

Let's start with no momentum to ensure monotone. Iterate:
```
while time:
   score_a, M, grad = smooth_data(a, eta)
   # if score_a > best_sc? shouldn't
   d=-grad/||grad||
   anorm=||a||
   for frac in sequence:
       a trial = project(a + frac*anorm*d)
       sc=score
       if sc < score_a: accept; break
   if accepted: a=a_trial; best update; step increase; else step decrease; if stuck perturb best.
   eta increase; 
```
This is coordinate-free subgradient. No momentum. Might be slower but safer. We can add a small momentum after many successes: maintain `a_prev`, use `y = a + 0.5*(a-a_prev)` but line search from y? To keep monotone, can use y only to compute direction, but update from a: `a_trial=project(a + frac*anorm*d_y)`. This damped.

Actually subgradient methods need many iterations; line search each iteration with 1-2 score trials. Cost: each iteration smooth_data includes FFT length Lg (16384) + trial score FFT Lc (8192) maybe 2-3. 10k iterations maybe okay. Use time check.

Step fraction: `anorm` of a (simplex) ~sqrt(1/n)≈0.014 for uniform; for smooth shape similar. `frac=0.1` => tau=0.0014 L2, small. Could use `frac=0.5` =>0.007, maybe good. Start 0.5, max 3. Projection. If too large, score worsens, reduce.

Eta schedule: `eta` softness. Start 5? Weight at 10% diff e^-0.5=0.6, many. Increase by 1.01 every 50 it to 200. If stuck, reset eta maybe lower.

But if using exact line search with score, maybe no need eta huge; can use active uniform: pick top K coefficients weights 1/K. We can choose K adaptively: find coefficients where c > M - ε. To avoid sorting, use eta. Start eta=5, end=100.

Let's write `project_simplex` efficiently:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    n = v.size
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    rho = np.nonzero(u * np.arange(1, n+1) > cssv - 1)[0][-1]
    theta = (cssv[rho] - 1) / (rho + 1)
    return np.maximum(v - theta, 0)
```
This returns sum 1. Ensure finite. Could use `np.partition` but fine.

`normalize` just clip/sum.

Candidate generation:
```
def make_candidates(n, rng, base=None):
    cands=[]
    if base finite: cands.append(normalize(base))
    cands.append(np.ones(n)/n)
    # beta grid
    xs=(np.arange(n)-0.5)/n
    for a_exp in [0,0.1,0.25,0.5,1,1.5,2,3]:
      for b_exp in [0,0.1,0.25,0.5,1,1.5,2,3]:
         val = ones
         if a_exp>0: val *= np.power(xs, a_exp)
         if b_exp>0: val *= np.power(1-xs, b_exp)
         cands.append(normalize(val))
    # exponentials
    for lam in [0.2,0.5,1,2,5,10]:
      for g in [0.5,1,2]:
         cands.append(normalize(np.exp(-lam*xs**g)))
         cands.append(normalize(np.exp(-lam*(1-xs)**g)))
    # random fourier
    for k in range(80):
       z = np.zeros(n)
       for j in range(1, 11):
          amp = rng.normal(0, 1.0/(j**0.8))
          z += amp * np.cos(2*np.pi*j*xs) + rng.normal(0, 1.0/(j**0.8))*np.sin(2*np.pi*j*xs)
       a = np.exp(z - z.max()); a/=a.sum(); cands.append(a)
    # maybe random positive smooth via splines
```
This creates ~80+64+20=160 candidates. Evaluating 160*FFT 8192 maybe ~0.1-0.3s? Good. But memory list 160*4806 ~7.7MB fine.

Pseudo:
```
def subgradient_refine(a0, n, budget, rng, eta_init=5, eta_max=300):
    deadline = time.monotonic()+budget
    a=normalize(a0)
    score_a, c, _ = fft_conv_score(a)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score_a
    eta=eta_init
    step_frac=0.1
    max_frac=2.0; min_frac=1e-4
    stuck=0
    iters=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       if score_a > best_score: # sync
          a=best_a.copy(); score_a=best_score
       # compute grad at current a (not momentum) for simplicity
       score_g, M, grad = smooth_data(a, eta)
       if not finite: break
       if score_g < best_score: best_score=score_g; best_a=a.copy()
       gn=np.linalg.norm(grad)
       if gn < 1e-18: break
       d = -grad/gn
       anorm=np.linalg.norm(a)
       base_tau = step_frac * max(anorm, 1e-6)
       accepted=False
       # try a short list: base_tau, base_tau*0.5, base_tau*0.2, maybe base_tau*1.5? For nonconvex, increasing step maybe; but we separately increase step_frac.
       for mult in (1.0, 0.5, 0.2, 0.1):
           cand = project_simplex(a + base_tau*mult*d)
           sc,tmp,_=fft_conv_score(cand)
           if sc < score_g - 1e-15:
               accepted=True; a_new=cand; sc_new=sc; break
       if accepted:
           a=a_new; score_a=sc_new
           if sc_new < best_score - 1e-12:
              best_score=sc_new; best_a=a.copy(); stuck=0
           else:
              stuck +=1
           step_frac = min(max_frac, step_frac*1.03)
           if stuck > 100:
              # sometimes reset eta lower or perturb
              if iters % 200 ==0:
                 a = best_a.copy()
                 a += rng.normal(0, 1e-5/np.sqrt(n), n)
                 a=normalize(a); score_a=fft_conv_score(a)[0]
              stuck=0
       else:
           step_frac *= 0.7
           if step_frac < min_frac:
               # reset to best with perturbation, fresh eta
               a = best_a.copy()
               a += rng.normal(0, 0.02 * np.sqrt(best_score/(2n))/np.sqrt(n)?, n) # too complex
               a=normalize(a)
               score_a=fft_conv_score(a)[0]
               step_frac=0.05
               eta=max(eta_init, eta*0.5)
               stuck=0
           stuck +=1
           if stuck > 100:
               # restart from best
               a=best_a.copy(); score_a=best_score; stuck=0; step_frac=0.05; eta=min(eta_max, eta*1.5)
       iters +=1
       # increase eta gradually
       if iters % 50 == 0:
           eta = min(eta_max, eta*1.02)
       if iters % 100 == 0:
           print(...)
    # final polish? return best
```

Basis L-BFGS:
```
def basis_obj_grad(c):
   z = B @ c
   a = exp(z - z.max()); a/=a.sum()
   sc, phi, ga = smooth_data_phi(a, eta)
   obj=2*n*phi
   # smoothed L-BFGS gradient wrt c:
   ag = a * (ga - np.dot(a, ga))
   gc = B.T @ ag
   return obj, gc
```

def basis_fun(c, B, n, eta):
    z = B @ c
    z = z - z.max()
    a = np.exp(z)
    s = a.sum()
    if s == 0: return 0, zeros
    a /= s
    sc, phi, ga = smooth_data_phi(a, eta)
    obj = 2.0*n*phi
    ag = a * (ga - np.dot(a, ga))
    gc = B.T @ ag
    return obj, gc
```

```
def smooth_data_a(a, eta):
   n=a.size; L=1 << ((3*n-3).bit_length())
   A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
   C=np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:2*n-1]
   M=C.max(); s=a.sum()
   score = 2*n*M/(s*s) if s>0 else inf
   if M <= 1e-300: ... uniform weights
   else:
      w=np.exp((C-M)*eta/M)
      ws=w.sum()
      if not finite or ws<=0: w=np.ones(C.size)/C.size; ws=1
      else: w/=ws
      W=np.fft.rfft(w,L)
      T=np.fft.irfft(W*np.conj(A), L)[:n]
      g=2*T
   # phi: M + (M/eta)*log(ws_unscaled) if ws before normalizing
   return score, phi, g
```

`smooth_data_a` with eta:
```
def smooth_data_a(a, eta):
  n=a.size
  L=1 << ((3*n - 3).bit_length())
  A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
  C=np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:2*n-1]
  s=a.sum()
  M=float(C.max())
  score = inf if s<1e-15 else 2*n*M/(s*s)
  if M <= 1e-300:
     w=np.ones(2*n-1)/ (2*n-1); ws=1; phi=0; grad=zeros
  else:
     t = (C-M)*eta/M
     # to avoid underflow? t max0 min maybe -1e6; exp underflows okay
     ew=np.exp(t)
     ws_unscaled=float(ew.sum())
     if not np.isfinite(ws_unscaled) or ws_unscaled<=0:
        w=np.ones(C.size)/C.size; ws_unscaled=C.size
     else:
        w=ew/ws_unscaled
     # phi = M + (M/eta)*log(ws_unscaled) because alpha=eta/M
     phi = M + (M / eta) * np.log(ws_unscaled) if eta>0 else M
     W=np.fft.rfft(w,L)
     T=np.fft.irfft(W*np.conj(A),L)[:n]
     grad=2*T
  return score, phi, grad
```
If eta=0, weights uniform? We'll not use 0.

Check gradient with normalized weights: If w uniform over 2n-1, T_j = sum a_r/(2n-1) ~1/n/(2n)=1/(2n²)?? grad ~1/n², small. For eta small, direction may be weak; but normalized direction unaffected. Good.

def normalize(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float).copy()
   a=np.maximum(a,0)
   s=a.sum()
   if not finite or s<=1e-15: return np.ones(a.size)/a.size
   a/=s
   return a
```

def subgradient_refine(a0, n, budget, rng, eta_init=10.0, eta_max=300.0):
    deadline = time.monotonic()+budget
    a = normalize(a0)
    score_a, _, _ = fft_conv_score(a)
    best_a = a.copy(); best_score = score_a
    eta = eta_init
    step_frac = 0.2
    max_step=2.0; min_step=1e-4
    stuck=0
    no_acc_count=0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       if it % 200 ==0: print(...)
       # If current score worsened due to numerical, reset to best if best better
       if best_score < score_a:
           # but we may be in a descent path; don't reset too often. If difference > 1e-8 after no acc? 
       score_g, phi, grad = smooth_data_a(a, eta)
       if not finite: a=best_a; score_a=best_score; continue
       if score_g < best_score:
           best_score=score_g; best_a=a.copy(); stuck=0
       gn=np.linalg.norm(grad)
       anorm=np.linalg.norm(a)
       accepted=False
       if gn > 1e-18:
          d=-grad/gn
          base=step_frac*max(anorm,1e-6)
          for mult in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125):
             cand=project_simplex(a + base*mult*d)
             sc,_,_=fft_conv_score(cand)
             if sc < score_g - 1e-14:
                accepted=True; a_new=cand; sc_new=sc; break
       if not accepted:
          # try single active peaks
          # Need c; recompute via fft_conv_score? We have C from smooth_data? smooth_data didn't return C. Could return C for fallback. Modify smooth_data_a to return C? That uses memory. Or in fallback compute C.
```

```
def propose_candidate(budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
  start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget_s-20
  try: base=np.array(height_sequence_1,float)

```
def basis_func(x, B, n, eta):
    z = B.dot(x)
    zmax = z.max()
    a = np.exp(z - zmax)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 0: return 1e100, np.zeros_like(x)
    a /= s
    score, phi, grad = smooth_data_a(a, eta)
    obj = 2.0*n*phi
    if not np.isfinite(obj) or not np.isfinite(grad).all():
       return 1e100, np.zeros_like(x)
    vec = a*(grad - np.dot(a, grad))
    jc = B.T.dot(vec)
    return obj, jc
```

Cheb basis top-level:
```
def cheb_basis(n,K):
   x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
   y=2*x-1
   B=np.zeros((n,K))
   if K==0: return B
   T0=np.ones(n); T1=y.copy()
   B[:,0]=T1
   if K==1: return B
   for j in range(2,K):
      Tj=2*y*T1 - T0
      B[:,j]=Tj
      T0,T1=T1,Tj
   return B
```
If K=0 no. For K=12, B columns T1..T12.

Let's define `fft_score(a)` efficiently:
```
def fft_score(a):
    n=a.size
    if n ==0: return inf
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return inf
    a=np.maximum(a,0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-12: return inf
    L=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    C=np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:2*n-1]
    M=float(np.max(C)) if C.size else 0
    return float(2*n*M/(s*s))
```

`choose_top`:
```
def choose_top(cands, scores, k):
   if not cands: return []
   order=np.argsort(scores)
   chosen=[]
   for idx in order:
      if len(chosen)>=k: break
      arr=cands[int(idx)]
      ok=True
      for other in chosen:
         if np.linalg.norm(arr - cands[other], ord=1) < 1e-3:
            ok=False; break
      if ok: chosen.append(int(idx))
   while len(chosen)<k:
       for idx in order:
          if idx not in chosen:
             chosen.append(int(idx)); break
       else: break
   return chosen
```
`idx` from np array int; compare okay.

Let's write `smooth_data_a` carefully:
```
def smooth_data_a(a, eta):
    n = a.size
    L = next_pow2(3*n - 3)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    full = np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)
    C = full[:2*n-1]
    s = a.sum()
    M = float(C.max())
    if s <= 1e-15:
       return np.inf, np.inf, np.zeros(n), C
    score = 2.0*n*M/(s*s)
    if M <= 1e-300 or eta <= 0:
       w = np.ones(C.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(C.size)
       ws = float(C.size)
       phi = M
    else:
       t = (C - M) * (eta / M)
       ew = np.exp(t)
       ws = float(ew.sum())
       if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
          w = np.ones(C.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(C.size)
          ws = float(C.size)
       else:
          w = ew / ws
       phi = M + (M / eta) * np.log(ws)
    W = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    T = np.fft.irfft(W * np.conj(A), L)[:n]
    grad = 2.0 * T
    return score, phi, grad, C
```
`next_pow2(3*n-3)`: if n=1, 0, next_pow2(0)? Our next_pow2 returns 1 for m<=1. Good.

`basis_func` uses `smooth_data_a(a, eta)` which returns C but heavy. It computes score even though objective phi uses phi. `score` maybe not needed but okay. `grad` is gradient phi. Good.

Subgradient line search uses `score_g` from smooth_data (exact score). Good.

Could `phi` overflow if M large? a sum1, M≤1 maybe; log ws≤log(2n)~10; M/eta small. fine.

```
def basis_refine(a, n, budget, eta=40.0, K=12):
    start=time.monotonic()
    a=normalize(a); best=a; bestsc=fft_score(a)
    if _scipy_minimize is None: return best,bestsc
    if budget < 30: return...
    B=cheb_basis(n,K)
    c0=init_basis_coeffs(a,B)
    maxiter = 20 if budget < 200 else 60
    res = _scipy_minimize(basis_func, c0, args=(B,n,eta), jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'maxls':20, 'ftol':1e-10, 'gtol':1e-6})
    if res.fun < bestsc:
       c=res.x; z=B@c; a2=softmax; sc=fft_score(a2); if finite and sc<bestsc: best=...
    return best,bestsc
```
But `res.fun` is smoothed score 2n phi, not exact; could be lower while exact higher. Reconstruct and check exact. Good.
Could run two K values: K=8 then K=14 if time. But precompute B K=14 and c0 with K? If use smaller K, B slice. To keep simple K=12. Maybe K=16 if n large. But L-BFGS 16 dims more eval. Use K=10. Let's choose K=10 for speed; smooth shape. But if need detail, full gradient. K=12 okay.

`maxiter` 30 maybe. Use options `maxfun`? L-BFGS-B has `maxfun`. Set `maxfun=200`. But maxfun not all? Use `maxiter=50, maxls=10`. Hard to ensure time. Good.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / sum(a)^2

The program combines:
  - analytic / random smooth initializations,
  - a projected subgradient line-search method using smoothed log-sum-exp
    approximations of the peak convolution constraint,
  - low-dimensional Chebyshev-basis polishing (scipy L-BFGS when available),
  - exact verification with the provided evaluate_sequence().

The returned sequence is a normal Python list of finite non-negative floats.
"""

import time
import numpy as np

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize as _scipy_minimize
except Exception:
    _scipy_minimize = None


def next_pow2(m: int) -> int:
    """Smallest power of two >= m (m >= 1)."""
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()


def normalize(a) -> np.ndarray:
    """Clip to non-negative and normalize to sum 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.ravel()
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    return a / s


def project_simplex(v: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Euclidean projection onto {x >= 0, sum(x) = 1}."""
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = v.size
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u * idx > (cssv - 1.0)
    if not np.any(cond):
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    rho = int(np.where(cond)[0][-1])
    theta = (cssv[rho] - 1.0) / float(rho + 1)
    return np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)


def fft_score(a: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """Fast exact score 2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / sum(a)^2."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return float("inf")
    L = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    C = np.fft.irfft(A * A, L)[: 2 * n - 1]
    M = float(C.max()) if C.size else 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * M / (s * s))


def smooth_data_a(a: np.ndarray, eta: float):
    """
    For smoothed peak objective phi_eta(a):
      eta is 'softness': weight ~ exp(eta*(c_k - max(c))/max(c)).
    Returns (exact_score, phi, gradient_of_phi, convolution_C).
    gradient_of_phi is grad of logsumexp(alpha*c)/alpha with alpha = eta/max(c).
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    L = next_pow2(3 * n - 3)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    C = np.fft.irfft(A * A, L)[: 2 * n - 1]
    s = float(a.sum())
    M = float(C.max()) if C.size else 0.0
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return float("inf"), float("inf"), np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64), C
    score = float(2.0 * n * M / (s * s))

    if M <= 1e-300 or eta <= 0.0:
        w = np.ones(C.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(C.size)
        ws = float(C.size)
        phi = M
    else:
        t = (C - M) * (float(eta) / M)
        ew = np.exp(t)
        ws = float(ew.sum())
        if (not np.isfinite(ws)) or ws <= 0.0:
            w = np.ones(C.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(C.size)
            ws = float(C.size)
        else:
            w = ew / ws
        phi = M + (M / float(eta)) * float(np.log(ws))

    W = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    T = np.fft.irfft(W * np.conj(A), L)[:n]
    grad = 2.0 * T
    return score, float(phi), grad, C


def direction_from_grad(grad: np.ndarray):
    """Project -grad onto the simplex tangent direction, unit norm."""
    d = -np.asarray(grad, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    d = d - float(d.mean())
    nrm = float(np.linalg.norm(d))
    if nrm <= 1e-18:
        return None
    return d / nrm


def gradient_single_peak(a: np.ndarray, k: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Gradient of the single convolution coefficient c_k wrt a."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
    hi = min(n, k + 1)
    if lo < hi:
        j = np.arange(lo, hi, dtype=np.int64)
        idx = k - j
        g[lo:hi] = 2.0 * a[idx]
    return g


def perturb(a: np.ndarray, rng: np.random.Generator, amp: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Add small Gaussian noise, clip, renormalize."""
    b = a + rng.standard_normal(a.size) * amp
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    s = float(b.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return a.copy()
    return b / s


def add_candidate(cands: list, scores: list, arr) -> None:
    """Normalize candidate and append if finite exact score."""
    a = normalize(arr)
    if a.size == 0:
        return
    sc = fft_score(a)
    if np.isfinite(sc):
        cands.append(a)
        scores.append(float(sc))


def generate_candidates(n: int, rng: np.random.Generator, base=None):
    """Create a pool of plausible non-negative sequences."""
    cands = []
    scores = []
    if n < 2:
        n = 4806

    if base is not None:
        b = np.asarray(base, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if b.size == n:
            add_candidate(cands, scores, b)

    add_candidate(cands, scores, np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    y = 1.0 - x

    # Beta-like shapes: x**a * y**b
    exps = [0.0, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0]
    for ae in exps:
        for be in exps:
            v = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
            if ae > 0.0:
                v *= np.power(x, ae)
            if be > 0.0:
                v *= np.power(y, be)
            add_candidate(cands, scores, v)

    # Exponential and power-exponential skews
    for lam in [0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0, 8.0]:
        for g in [0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0]:
            add_candidate(cands, scores, np.exp(-lam * np.power(x, g)))
            add_candidate(cands, scores, np.exp(-lam * np.power(y, g)))

    # Gaussian-mixtures
    for _ in range(80):
        w1 = float(rng.uniform(0.15, 0.85))
        c1 = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.70))
        s1 = float(rng.uniform(0.03, 0.45))
        c2 = float(rng.uniform(0.30, 0.95))
        s2 = float(rng.uniform(0.03, 0.45))
        v = w1 * np.exp(-(((x - c1) / s1) ** 2)) + (1.0 - w1) * np.exp(-(((x - c2) / s2) ** 2))
        add_candidate(cands, scores, v)

    # Random smooth non-periodic shapes via bounded Chebyshev random Fourier-ish basis
    for _ in range(100):
        z = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for j in range(1, 13):
            amp1 = float(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0 / (j ** 0.75)))
            amp2 = float(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0 / (j ** 0.75)))
            z += amp1 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * j * x) + amp2 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * j * x)
        z -= z.max()
        v = np.exp(z)
        v += 1e-8
        add_candidate(cands, scores, v)

    # A few random positive low-degree polynomials / rational skews
    for _ in range(40):
        c0 = float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.5))
        c1 = float(rng.normal(0.0, 2.0))
        c2 = float(rng.normal(0.0, 3.0))
        c3 = float(rng.normal(0.0, 6.0))
        v = c0 + c1 * x + c2 * x * x + c3 * x ** 3
        v = np.maximum(v, 0.0)
        if v.sum() <= 0:
            v = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        add_candidate(cands, scores, v)

        v2 = 1.0 / (1.0 + float(rng.uniform(0.0, 8.0)) * np.power(x, float(rng.uniform(0.25, 2.0))))
        add_candidate(cands, scores, v2)

    return cands, scores


def choose_top(cands: list, scores: list, k: int) -> list:
    """Choose up to k distinct candidates by score."""
    if not cands:
        return []
    order = np.argsort(scores)
    chosen = []
    for idx in order:
        idx = int(idx)
        if len(chosen) >= k:
            break
        ok = True
        for j in chosen:
            if j == idx:
                continue
            if float(np.linalg.norm(cands[idx] - cands[j], ord=1)) < 1e-3:
                ok = False
                break
        if ok:
            chosen.append(idx)
    # Fill if duplicates eliminated too much.
    for idx in order:
        idx = int(idx)
        if len(chosen) >= k:
            break
        if idx not in chosen:
            chosen.append(idx)
    return chosen


def mix_top_candidates(cands: list, scores: list, top_idx: list) -> None:
    """Add pairwise convex combinations of top candidates."""
    m = min(len(top_idx), 5)
    weights = [0.15, 0.30, 0.50, 0.70, 0.85]
    for ii in range(m):
        for jj in range(ii + 1, m):
            i = top_idx[ii]
            j = top_idx[jj]
            for w in weights:
                mix = w * cands[i] + (1.0 - w) * cands[j]
                add_candidate(cands, scores, mix)


def subgradient_refine(a0: np.ndarray, n: int, budget: float, rng: np.random.Generator,
                       eta_init: float = 8.0, eta_max: float = 300.0):
    """
    Projected line-search subgradient method using softly-weighted active
    convolution peaks.  Improves the exact objective as far as possible in budget.
    """
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, float(budget))
    a = normalize(a0)
    score_a = fft_score(a)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score_a

    eta = float(eta_init)
    step_frac = 0.25
    min_step = 1e-5
    max_step = 2.5
    stuck = 0
    no_acc = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if it % 250 == 0:
            print(f"      subgrad it={it} score={best_score:.8f} eta={eta:.2f} step={step_frac:.3f}", flush=True)

        # If current point drifted far above the known best, reset.
        if best_score + 1e-8 < score_a and it > 30:
            a = best_a.copy()
            score_a = best_score
            step_frac = 0.25
            no_acc = 0

        score_g, phi, grad, C = smooth_data_a(a, eta)
        if (not np.isfinite(score_g)) or (not np.isfinite(phi)):
            a = best_a.copy()
            score_a = best_score
            step_frac = 0.25
            continue

        if score_g < best_score:
            best_score = score_g
            best_a = a.copy()
            stuck = 0

        accepted = False
        a_new = None
        sc_new = None
        anorm = float(np.linalg.norm(a))
        if not np.isfinite(anorm) or anorm <= 0:
            break
        base_tau = step_frac * anorm

        d = direction_from_grad(grad)
        if d is not None:
            for mult in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125):
                cand = project_simplex(a + base_tau * mult * d)
                sc = fft_score(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < score_g - 1e-15:
                    accepted = True
                    a_new = cand
                    sc_new = sc
                    break

        # If the soft combined direction does not help, try a few pure active-peak subgradients.
        if (not accepted) and C.size >= 3:
            topk = np.argpartition(C, -3)[-3:]
            for kk in topk:
                gk = gradient_single_peak(a, int(kk))
                dk = direction_from_grad(gk)
                if dk is None:
                    continue
                for mult in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25):
                    cand = project_simplex(a + base_tau * mult * dk)
                    sc = fft_score(cand)
                    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < score_g - 1e-15:
                        accepted = True
                        a_new = cand
                        sc_new = sc
                        break
                if accepted:
                    break

        if accepted:
            a = a_new
            score_a = float(sc_new)
            if score_a < best_score:
                best_score = score_a
                best_a = a.copy()
                stuck = 0
            else:
                stuck += 1
            no_acc = 0
            step_frac = min(max_step, step_frac * 1.03)
            if it % 40 == 0:
                eta = min(eta_max, eta * 1.04)
        else:
            no_acc += 1
            step_frac *= 0.7
            if step_frac < min_step or no_acc > 6 or stuck > 120:
                amp = 0.015 / np.sqrt(max(1, n))
                a = perturb(best_a, rng, amp)
                score_a = fft_score(a)
                step_frac = 0.2
                eta = min(eta_max, max(float(eta_init), eta * 1.15))
                no_acc = 0
                stuck = 0
                it += 1
                continue

        it += 1
        if it % 250 == 0 and stuck > 40:
            eta = max(float(eta_init), eta * 0.85)

    # Final exact check.
    sc = fft_score(a)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
        best_score = sc
        best_a = a.copy()
    return best_a, best_score


def cheb_basis(n: int, K: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Chebyshev basis T_j(2*x-1), j=1..K, on x in (0,1).
    Avoids constant term because the objective is scale-invariant.
    """
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    t = 2.0 * x - 1.0
    B = np.zeros((n, K), dtype=np.float64)
    if K <= 0:
        return B
    T0 = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    T1 = t.astype(np.float64, copy=True)
    B[:, 0] = T1
    for j in range(1, K):
        Tj = 2.0 * t * T1 - T0
        B[:, j] = Tj
        T0, T1 = T1, Tj
    return B


def init_basis_coeffs(a: np.ndarray, B: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Least-squares fit log(a) by the Chebyshev basis."""
    a = normalize(a)
    z = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-14))
    z = z - float(z.mean())
    if B.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    c, *_ = np.linalg.lstsq(B, z, rcond=None)
    return np.clip(c, -40.0, 40.0)


def basis_func(c: np.ndarray, B: np.ndarray, n: int, eta: float):
    """
    Objective + gradient for low-dimensional Chebyshev parameters.
    a = softmax(B@c), objective is smoothed 2n*phi_eta(a).
    """
    z = B.dot(c)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
        return 1e100, np.zeros_like(c, dtype=np.float64)
    z = z - float(z.max())
    a = np.exp(z)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return 1e100, np.zeros_like(c, dtype=np.float64)
    a /= s

    score, phi, ga, _ = smooth_data_a(a, eta)
    obj = 2.0 * n * phi
    if (not np.isfinite(obj)) or (not np.all(np.isfinite(ga))):
        return 1e100, np.zeros_like(c, dtype=np.float64)

    ag = a * (ga - float(np.dot(a, ga)))
    gc = B.T.dot(ag)
    gc = np.nan_to_num(gc, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return float(obj), gc


def basis_refine(a0: np.ndarray, n: int, budget: float, eta: float = 50.0, K: int = 12):
    """
    Polish the smooth envelope of a candidate in a low-dimensional Chebyshev basis.
    Returns best (array, exact_score) found.
    """
    a = normalize(a0)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = fft_score(a)
    if _scipy_minimize is None or budget < 15.0:
        return best_a, best_score

    B = cheb_basis(n, K)
    c0 = init_basis_coeffs(a, B)

    maxiter = 25
    if budget > 120.0:
        maxiter = 45
    if budget > 300.0:
        maxiter = 70

    try:
        res = _scipy_minimize(
            basis_func,
            c0,
            args=(B, n, float(eta)),
            jac=True,
            method="L-BFGS-B",
            options={
                "maxiter": maxiter,
                "maxls": 16,
                "ftol": 1e-12,
                "gtol": 1e-7,
            },
        )
    except Exception:
        return best_a, best_score

    if res is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)):
        z = B.dot(res.x)
        z = z - float(z.max())
        b = np.exp(z)
        s = float(b.sum())
        if s > 1e-15 and np.all(np.isfinite(b)):
            b = b / s
            sc = fft_score(b)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_a = b.copy()
                best_score = sc
    return best_a, best_score


def propose_candidate(budget_s: float = 2400.0, **kwargs):
    """
    Main entry point.  Searches for a good non-negative sequence and returns it
    as a Python list of floats.
    """
    total_start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = total_start + max(60.0, float(budget_s)) - 18.0

    # Try to use the previously found construction as a strong seed.
    base = None
    n = 4806
    try:
        base_seq = height_sequence_1  # noqa: F821 (provided by harness)
        arr = np.asarray(base_seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size >= 2 and arr.size <= 12000:
            n = int(arr.size)
            base = arr
    except Exception:
        base = None
        n = 4806

    if n < 2:
        n = 4806

    rng = np.random.default_rng(20240521)

    print(f"=== search start: n={n} budget={float(budget_s):.0f}s ===", flush=True)

    # --- Build initial pool ---
    cands, scores = generate_candidates(n, rng, base)
    if not cands:
        u = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        cands = [u]
        scores = [float(fft_score(u))]

    # Pairwise mixing of best current seeds.
    top0 = choose_top(cands, scores, 5)
    mix_top_candidates(cands, scores, top0)

    # Best exact among the pool.
    bidx = int(np.argmin(scores))
    best_a = cands[bidx].copy()
    best_score = float(scores[bidx])
    print(f"initial best: {best_score:.8f}", flush=True)

    # If the previous seed is excellent, add its optimized version as an explicit start.
    starts = []
    top = choose_top(cands, scores, 5)
    if base is not None:
        starts.append(cands[0] if len(cands) > 0 else best_a.copy())
    for idx in top:
        if idx < len(cands) and cands[idx] not in starts:
            starts.append(cands[idx])
    if len(starts) == 0:
        starts.append(best_a.copy())

    # --- Main optimization over several starts ---
    # Run a quick Chebyshev polish on the best initial seed, then propagrate it as a start.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 120 and _scipy_minimize is not None:
        a_tmp, sc_tmp = basis_refine(best_a, n, min(100.0, rem - 30.0), eta=40.0, K=10)
        if np.isfinite(sc_tmp) and sc_tmp < best_score:
            best_score = sc_tmp
            best_a = a_tmp.copy()
            starts.append(a_tmp.copy())
            print(f"early basis polish: {best_score:.8f}", flush=True)

    # Allocate time among starts, with a cap so one bad start does not consume all budget.
    remaining_starts = [s for s in starts if s is not None]
    if remaining_starts:
        # Remove duplicate starts (rough L1).
        unique_starts = []
        for s in remaining_starts:
            ok = True
            for u in unique_starts:
                if float(np.linalg.norm(s - u, ord=1)) < 1e-4:
                    ok = False
                    break
            if ok:
                unique_starts.append(s)
        starts = unique_starts

    limit = max(1, len(starts))
    for si, start in enumerate(starts):
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 45.0:
            break
        share = rem / float(limit - si)
        chunk = float(min(700.0, max(60.0, share)))
        # Reserve a little if this is one of the first runs and scipy is available.
        basis_chunk = 0.0
        if _scipy_minimize is not None and chunk > 240.0 and si < 3:
            basis_chunk = min(120.0, max(40.0, 0.2 * chunk))
            a_tmp, sc_tmp = basis_refine(start, n, basis_chunk, eta=35.0, K=10)
            if np.isfinite(sc_tmp) and sc_tmp < best_score:
                best_score = sc_tmp
                best_a = a_tmp.copy()
                chunk -= basis_chunk
                start = a_tmp
            else:
                chunk -= basis_chunk
                start = a_tmp

        if chunk > 20.0:
            a_tmp, sc_tmp = subgradient_refine(start, n, chunk, rng, eta_init=8.0, eta_max=320.0)
            if np.isfinite(sc_tmp) and sc_tmp < best_score:
                best_score = sc_tmp
                best_a = a_tmp.copy()
        print(f"start {si+1}/{len(starts)} best: {best_score:.8f}", flush=True)

    # --- Final polishing ---
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 90 and _scipy_minimize is not None:
        a_tmp, sc_tmp = basis_refine(best_a, n, min(240.0, rem - 30.0), eta=70.0, K=12)
        if np.isfinite(sc_tmp) and sc_tmp < best_score:
            best_score = sc_tmp
            best_a = a_tmp.copy()
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()

    if rem > 60:
        a_tmp, sc_tmp = subgradient_refine(best_a, n, rem - 10.0, rng, eta_init=50.0, eta_max=600.0)
        if np.isfinite(sc_tmp) and sc_tmp < best_score:
            best_score = sc_tmp
            best_a = a_tmp.copy()

    print(f"post-polish best: {best_score:.8f}", flush=True)

    # Ensure final object is a clean list of non-negative finite floats.
    final_arr = normalize(best_a)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(final_arr)):
        final_arr = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    final_list = [float(max(0.0, x)) for x in final_arr]

    # Final verification with the official evaluator.
    fallback_list = final_list
    try:
        final_score = float(evaluate_sequence(final_list))  # noqa: F821
    except Exception:
        final_score = float(fft_score(final_arr))

    if (not np.isfinite(final_score)) or final_score > best_score + 1e-7:
        # Try the first safe candidate from the pool, then uniform.
        for cand in cands[:8]:
            try:
                trial = [float(max(0.0, float(x))) for x in normalize(cand)]
                tr_score = float(evaluate_sequence(trial))  # noqa: F821
                if np.isfinite(tr_score) and tr_score < final_score:
                    final_list = trial
                    final_score = tr_score
                    break
            except Exception:
                continue
        else:
            try:
                trial = [1.0] * n
                tr_score = float(evaluate_sequence(trial))  # noqa: F821
                if np.isfinite(tr_score):
                    final_list = trial
                    final_score = tr_score
            except Exception:
                final_list = [1.0] * n
                final_score = float("inf")

    print(f"=== FINAL returned score: {final_score:.8f} (n={len(final_list)}) ===", flush=True)
    return final_list
```
